## Compiler admission contract, before dependent generation

The proposed separate rule is **compiler-constant-using-v1**. Neither the
restricted v2 parser nor any historical capture/review is replaced. Initial
compiler output is candidate provenance only until independent review and
Rust/artifact admission are implemented. Registration, compiler observation,
assertion correspondence and source/Rust execution remain distinct evidence.

1. Install the tracer only around registration in the complete isolated clean
   pinned checkout. Capture `:start` lexical source, remote/imported macro calls
   and `:on_module` bytes/definitions. Each module version keeps actual compiled
   namespace, source path/hash, definition metadata, BEAM SHA-256 and sequence.
   A `__using__/1` call binds to the exact preceding compiled version; verify
   the currently loaded code fingerprint also matches that version. A later
   same-name redefinition cannot retroactively change an earlier use. Unknown
   preloaded targets or missing/currently different definitions stay unresolved.
2. Static-decode only a single guardless `defmacro __using__/1` clause with one
   variable binder whose return consists entirely of literal data and
   tuple/list constructors. No evaluation, macro expansion or macro invocation
   occurs in matching. Calls, variables, unquote/splicing, computed returns,
   guarded/multiple clauses and unknown data forms stay unresolved. Compare
   callback-time definitions with the captured BEAM debug definition as well.
   The constant return proves the parameter is unused; neither `_` nor `_opts`
   is a special admission name.
3. Associate the decoded complete quote with one direct source `__using__`
   quote at the compiler-observed definition line in the exact hashed source.
   Compare the entire normalized quote/body, preserving literal values,
   operators, calls, order and names. Remove compiler-only AST location/import
   metadata and normalize quote hygiene context; record the normalization rule
   and both body hashes. Do not use the source's lexical module spelling to
   override the actual compiled namespace. Dynamic/nested/ambiguous declarations
   or missing/full-body disagreement remain unresolved.
4. Require an exact runtime consumer path/line/module/name from real
   `__ex_unit__/0` registration, its compiled module version, one exact direct
   literal source `use`, the compiler-resolved used module/version and the
   unique literal test/describe declaration. Explicit calls to `__using__/1`
   are outside this admission grammar. Every observed test/describe/setup/
   setup_all call at that use location and expansion interval must resolve
   directly to the captured installed ExUnit.Case BEAM/version and fingerprint;
   require an actual test macro call too. One genuine Case call beneath a
   foreign delegating macro is insufficient. This restricts the current proof,
   rather than admitting framework scaffolding by name.
   Unknown nested expansion, conflicting same-line calls, changed registration,
   absent test trace or duplicate declaration remains unresolved. Test/setup
   callbacks are never run by collection/matching.
5. The separate schema retains ordered call/module evidence, raw original
   identities/tags, full pinned/source/tool/compiler/OTP/BEAM references,
   collector and helper hashes, per-helper/final profiles, fixture/setup
   non-execution and exact linked/unmatched/frozen complements. Preserve copied
   supplemental-source review. Hashes bind recorded bytes, not authorship or
   semantic completeness. Immutable historical files remain untouched.

Tracer work is tooling only, bounded and separate from runtime dependencies.
Cache source/compiled-version observations rather than reparsing per assertion;
retain original BEAM/proof data for independent verification. Malformed or
oversized proof inputs fail closed. Tooling/test files remain <=600 physical
lines; production architecture and 400-line limits do not change.

API used by the transferred controls: `start(source_root)`, `snapshot()` and
`match(raw_case, snapshot, declarations)`. Missing/tampered proof and ambiguous
consumer/declaration identities must return unresolved, not throw or guess.
The new capture will not change `workflow-progress.json` until producer,
proof/schema, adversarial Rust admission and exact semantics are independently
reviewed. No old unproved origin is restored by relabeling it.

Primary API support was checked against the pinned
[Code source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/elixir-lang/elixir/v1.19.5/lib/elixir/lib/code.ex)
and [ExUnit.Case source](https://github.com/elixir-lang/elixir/blob/v1.19.5/lib/ex_unit/lib/ex_unit/case.ex).
The tracer observes compiler-resolved macro targets and on-module bytecode;
the actual local spike and regression controls, rather than documentation or
a version-name assumption, establish this increment's behavior.

## Per-case catalog and execution contract

Catalog every original raw runtime identity and every frozen declaration,
including unresolved/generated identities and reviewed reference-only entries.
Keep an explicit per-case scope disposition, exact source assertion navigation,
fixture/setup/helper dependencies, effective tags and required execution
profiles. Initially unknown scope/dependencies remain pending; no filename or
whole mixed suite is an exclusion. Include governance, approvals, budgets,
effects, steps and shared durability/cluster/API guarantees under the current
workflow-only scope assumption. Unrelated KV/Redis/UI/Elixir-specific cases
require an actual source/assertion review and explicit reason before exclusion.

Passing requires complete original observations mapped to actual Rust
counterparts with scenario/harness identity and independently recorded execution
for every mandatory profile. Partial existing mappings, ignored/deferred cases,
registration, generated catalog rows and synthetic compiler controls never
become passing whole cases. Missing fixtures/profile execution and unclassified
or unresolved identities keep the full gate closed. Actual selected-source
execution already recorded in prior notes remains separate subset evidence.

## Initial actual compiler control results

The first implementation followed all four actual compiled alias targets to the
second source/quote with matching full-body and BEAM hashes. Its unchanged
transferred controls then failed **4/6**, because ExUnit's generated test wrapper
returns `:ok`, rather than the last body value. Root accepted the concrete
fixture correction: preserve its assertions/body/source identity, emit a body
marker message, assert wrapper `:ok` and observe the exact `:second` marker.
No original source/capture changed. Actual initial output is
`/private/tmp/ferricstore2-completion-parity-compiler-lineage-first.log`.

Added four proof controls before dependent fixes. Actual behavior RED:
**10 tests, two failures**, exit **2**, seed 420448,
`/private/tmp/ferricstore2-completion-parity-compiler-fingerprint-red.log`.
The prior reader accepted changed loaded-code fingerprints and an invented
module proof ID with consistent event references. The fix compares captured
fingerprints to BEAM code fingerprints, binds use-event fingerprints to that
module version, recomputes proof IDs from the actual compiled namespace,
source/hash/BEAM/hash/sequence, and validates framework code metadata.
Missing genuine Case traces, malformed BEAM bytes, ambiguous declarations,
unknown/dynamic constructors and nested quote directives remain unresolved.

Actual GREEN after those fixes: **10 tests, zero failures**, exit **0**,
seed 923265, `/private/tmp/ferricstore2-completion-parity-compiler-fingerprint-green.log`.
Owned Elixir formatting is applied only to the new compiler files;
the formatted control rerun is recorded separately. Producer/schema capture
and exhaustive pending catalog follow without changing historical accounting.

## Additional actual controls and reciprocal findings

An explicit remote `__using__/1` invocation compiled real registered tests, but

The source-use index now binds the compiler's actual consumer definition line
to one direct literal use line. During that fix the BEAM reader initially read

reader obtains its line through `:erl_anno.line/1`. Final actual **11/0** green,
seed 826223, is `/private/tmp/ferricstore2-completion-parity-compiler-literal-use-green.log`.

Performance's actual independent run reproduced the same 11/1 failure and
reported **P-L1**: the first implementation reparsed source and decoded BEAMs
per runtime case. The new `match_all/3` prepares fresh verified source analyses
once per physical source and BEAM analyses once per bound content hash, then
indexes module versions, exact registrations, use locations and declarations.
It rechecks source bytes, source and BEAM digests, compiled file/namespace,
definition metadata, fingerprints and recomputed proof IDs on each batch.
The cache preserves duplicates for ambiguity rejection and does not retain
caller trust across batches. This removes the observed repeated analyses;
no capture throughput or timing improvement is claimed. The refactored actual
11/0 run, seed 217569, is
`/private/tmp/ferricstore2-completion-parity-compiler-cached-green.log`.

## Candidate schema and finite handoff

`tests/parity/workflow-compiler-lineage.schema.json` is the concrete proposed
external contract for a separate capture. It fixes rule/version, pinned source
and manifest, normalization `elixir-ast-metadata-hygiene-etf-v1`, and
`admission: proposal-only`. Source/BEAM/tool/collector/framework artifacts are
hash-bound references; exact registration and collection details retain raw
identities, tags, helpers and profiles without rewriting historical bytes.
Module versions include actual compiled namespace/definition line, BEAM/source
hashes, fingerprint and definition digest. Ordered call rows retain resolved
target, use proof, fingerprint and genuine-framework observations. Candidates
retain both complete normalized quote hashes and exact consumer/registration/
declaration navigation. Unresolved raw IDs are an explicit complement.

JSON shape alone is insufficient. The future external serializer/reader must
verify referenced bytes and sizes, all proof IDs, sequence/namespace/version
relations, complete exact raw identity and declaration complements, duplicate
rejection, current producer/compiler versions, frozen manifest navigation,
helper/profile provenance and full-body equality before admitting a candidate.
Current controls use the in-memory snapshot producer and verifier. External
capture serialization, Rust admission/controls and the exhaustive per-case
catalog remain follow-up work; no new source capture or ledger regeneration
was performed in this finite handoff.

The independent reader review must also audit every event/registration field,
including the observed use kind/target namespace and the consumer proof's
namespace/path relation. The in-memory producer supplies those observations;
passing the represented controls is not a certificate that arbitrary fabricated
event rows satisfy all proposed external relations. Additional concrete
tampering controls belong before any external admission.

The grammar is deliberately incomplete. A real policy section's direct quote
begins with `alias Ferricstore.Test.ShardHelpers`; such quote directives are
currently unresolved. Setup macros resolving to another framework module also
need their own exact authority proof. The named-binder control proves the
represented compiler form, not successful recovery of the real source suite.
There are no new admitted origins and no new original test executions. The
historical 5,903/4,222/0/1,681/1,655 accounting and 37/38/124 partial ledger are
unchanged. Compiler controls do not close any mandatory execution profile.

After the current proof/schema is stable, root requested a natural finite
handoff for independent architecture review, followed by a new isolated capture
and case catalog. Full completion remains active beyond this handoff. Peer
production files, source-linked test bodies and immutable reference inputs
were not edited by this owner.

Final owner light checks: schema parses as JSON with proposal-only admission;
all six owned files have no trailing whitespace; global saved-tree
`git diff --check` exits 0. Current tooling sizes are producer 172, data 249,
verifier 243 and controls 310 physical lines; schema 139 and this note remain
below 600 too. These are format/size checks, not external schema-instance
admission or original execution. No Cargo build, new dependency, frozen edit,
commit or push was performed by this owner in the compiler phase.

## Mandatory architecture gate for new artifacts

Before any new file/type/class/module/model, consult dedicated architecture and
wait for recorded artifact/named-batch approval; broad ownership is insufficient.
Existing relation fixes preceded this gate. The proposed exact serializer/
capture/verifier/reader paths, names, APIs/dependencies/resource plan were sent
before creation; read-only validation continued during natural reviewer rotation.

### Concrete batch clarification for architecture decision

Requested files/modules: `scripts/compiler-lineage-artifact.exs`
(`WorkflowCompilerLineageArtifact`, bounded data writer/reader),
`scripts/collect-compiler-lineage.exs` (`WorkflowCompilerCapture`, registration),
`scripts/verify-compiler-lineage.exs` (`WorkflowCompilerProofCheck`, pure CLI),
`scripts/tests/compiler-artifact-test.exs` (`WorkflowCompilerArtifactTest`).
Requested Rust paths: `src/compiler_lineage.rs`,
`src/compiler_lineage/{model,relations,refs,verifier}.rs` and
`tests/compiler_lineage.rs`, all under `crates/wf-parity-tests`. They have no
runtime/domain dependency or new package. Existing source/metadata models are
read without editing frozen captures. No hidden validity flag confers admission.

The exact final capture root is `tests/parity/collection/compiler-v1/`:
`compiler-lineage.json`, `collection-details.json`, `runtime-registration.json`,
`sources/<sha256>.exs`, and `beams/<sha256>.beam`. SHA names use lowercase
64-hex SHA-256 of actual content. Native framework/compiler bytecode uses the
same BEAM store. The unchanged collector additionally writes its two explicitly
reviewed pinned copies at `source-supplement/<source_path>`; those exact source
paths are in `collection-root-review.json`, not an unrestricted output tree.
There are no copied source execution files or passed-case report artifacts.
Collection stages in `tests/parity/collection/compiler-v1-staging-<pid>-<nonce>/`
with the same bounded names. Incomplete staging is never read by admission.
Only a fully checked complete stage is renamed to the absent final root;
existing capture roots are never overwritten. Reader paths reject traversal,
symlinks and unknown/missing/extra capture contents.

`Capture` exactly mirrors strict schema fields plus the explicitly proposed
limits/current-input/compiler bindings: pinned source/manifest, source checkout,
Elixir version/build info, OTP, normalization, exact raw/exact-registration
references, exact tool references, `mix.lock` bytes/hash, launcher path/hash,
framework and compiler fingerprints, ordered module/call rows, complete raw
registered rows, candidates and explicit unresolved raw/declaration complements.
`ModuleProof`, `TraceCall`, `ObservedCase`, `CandidateOrigin`,
`CaptureRequirements` and `FrameworkProof` mirror their schema dictionaries.
FrameworkProof is also the shared module/BEAM/fingerprint representation for
the native Code/Module/Macro/JSON and internal compiler components. This adds
compiler inputs without another unlisted model. Existing `ArtifactRef` is
reused; actual sizes are read and checked, not trusted from metadata.
`CompilerLineageSummary` reports raw/candidate/unresolved/version/call counts
only, with proposal-only and non-execution wording.

`CaptureLimits` owns fixed tooling profile values: `content_bytes=16777216`,
`aggregate_content_bytes=268435456`, `capture_metadata_bytes=67108864`,
`metadata_bytes=67108864`, `module_versions=10000`, `calls=1000000`,
`runtime_cases=100000`, `stdout_bytes=1048576`, `stderr_bytes=1048576`,
`verifier_deadline_ms=60000`. These values cannot be raised by input metadata.
The existing producer interns actual equal source/BEAM binaries and charges
unique content plus encoded metadata/counts before ETS retention; serializer
interning alone is too late. External reads charge actual unique content and
bounded rows before AST/BEAM analysis. Hash/byte/path equality is verified even
for repeated references; deduplication cannot hide a changed duplicate file.
These are logical storage/work bounds, not native RSS or macro sandbox claims.

Rust resolves the current `elixir` launcher from PATH, canonicalizes it and
checks its recorded byte hash before execution. It passes the checked absolute
current verifier path, canonical Rust root and portable capture metadata path
as separate `std::process::Command` arguments, with root cwd and null stdin.
There is no shell command string, interpolation or caller-supplied script.
Trusted tooling `.exs` runs normally; copied source is only parsed to AST and
copied BEAM/debug definitions are only inspected as data, never compiled,
evaluated, loaded or invoked. Verification uses Elixir's built-in JSON module,
not an unbound Jason load path. Exact current tool hashes, compiler/OTP/build,
native compiler/framework fingerprints, frozen/source/lock/helper/profile/input
bindings and complete raw/tag/declaration complements are prerequisites.

Bounded child stdout/stderr use uniquely created files under
`<system-temp>/wf-compiler-verify-<pid>-<counter>/{stdout,stderr}`; these exact
generated temporary outputs are included in the requested batch. Poll child
exit, output file sizes and the 60-second deadline; kill/wait on overflow or
timeout, read at most each limit plus one, and remove only the directory created
by this call. A deadline/tool/prerequisite failure is an operational verification
failure, never a durable business result. Nonzero exit, output excess, malformed
or multiple report objects, version drift and missing tools stay closed.

`PureVerification` is strict report schema1: capture SHA-256, source commit,
Elixir version, OTP, normalization, raw/candidate/unresolved counts and
`statistics: VerificationStats`. `VerificationStats` has source_reads,
source_parses, beam_decodes, registered_cases, module_versions and macro_events;
all are independently recomputed and cross-checked, not execution statuses.
Rust structural checks alone never admit a macro origin: trusted current pure
verification must reconstruct exact constant quotes and all relations and exit0.
The check CLI keeps all output proposal-only and leaves old progress unchanged.
Missing verifier/current-input mismatch/helper tamper/zero raw capture/nonzero
exit/output excess/deadline and version drift controls precede acceptance.

Static batch/path review precedes creation; original capture separately awaits
stable producer/schema/controls and its explicit approval/review gate.

Read recorded **A-N4** approval before static batch/model creation. Original
capture and unlisted fixtures remain withheld; proposed fixture/output names
awaited their separate exact approval. Conditions: pre-ETS interning/charging,
inflated debug-ETF bounds/stream completion, bounded safe subprocess outputs
and sanitized startup/code-path environment.
The verifier removes ERL_FLAGS/ERL_AFLAGS/ERL_ZFLAGS/ERL_LIBS/
ELIXIR_ERL_OPTIONS/ERL_INETRC injection inputs and checks current native code
fingerprints. Trusted tooling execution remains distinct from compiling or
executing copied source/BEAM definitions.

Independently reviewed performance's actual 19/19 relation recheck and unchanged
six-mutation probe output. P-L1 fresh batch caches are structurally resolved;
P-L3 aggregate capture limits remain implementation work. Its lower-ranked
literal-test prefix-appending cost observation is accurate source analysis,
not measured capture throughput. Reviewed the matching-release E1 note's
two-run samples, warmup/profile equality and retained variation; spare-capacity
conversion costs are local observations and imply no service/RSS/clone claim.

The compressed native Case debug control then reproduced an actual expansion

Propose one concrete **new tooling helper only**:
scripts/case-metadata.exs, module WorkflowCaseMetadata, <=600 physical lines.
It owns bounded metadata admission, primitive normalization, canonical JSON
measurement/emission and exclusive creation of exactly the raw JSON pair.
No new struct/type/class/model/exception/alias/dependency or runtime crate edge.
State is a documented ordinary tuple {runtime_cases, visited_nodes,
retained_external_bytes}; it carries counts, not trusted validity/authority.
Public functions are exactly limits/0, new_budget/0, admit/2, admit_case/2,
runtime_header/3, failure/5, measure_pair/2 and write_pair/4. Proposed private
functions are scan/4, normalize/1, json_key/1, bounded_count/2, measure_json/3,
escape_size/1, scalar_json/1, emit_json/4, emit_chunk/3, prepare_output/2,
open_pair/2 and finish_or_cleanup/3. Helper depends only on existing
compiler-lineage-data.exs plus std/OTP/native JSON; no Mix/application/ExUnit
test dispatch, source compilation, Jason protocol call or verifier override.
Any necessary additional function/file/model must be consulted before creation.
admit/2 and admit_case/2 return {normalized_value, next_budget}; failure/5
returns {diagnostic_string, next_budget}. Validate nonnegative bounded tuple
components on entry; the tuple cannot raise limits or certify completeness.
runtime_header/3 returns the existing four physical identity fields and does
not independently retain tags. write_pair/4 returns the two actual byte counts
and SHA256 values only after complete measured emission; these are output
observations, never acceptance flags. No public collection/verification facade.

Fixed prospective limits (requiring architecture/performance approval, not a
claim of actual full-source feasibility):100,000 admitted runtime rows,10,000
registered modules,20,000 source/helper/root entries, depth96,200,000 nodes per
single intake value,4,000,000 visited nodes across the collector's admitted
metadata,16MiB per scalar and per prospective compact normalized row/value,
64MiB sum of actual admitted normalized external_size values. Source reads
have8MiB manifest/review and16MiB other-file caps,20,000 read occurrences and
256MiB actual summed read bytes, charged before hashing/copying. JSON pair
measurement/emission has8,000,000 occurrence visits, depth96 and64MiB actual
combined UTF-8 bytes including both newlines; every emitted chunk <=64KiB.
Integers are restricted to exact signed-i64/unsigned-u64 JSON values, finite
float tokens are measured with the native scalar encoder, and normalized map
keys must be unique valid UTF-8. These are explicit new producer work profiles;
caller metadata cannot raise them. The existing compiler's64MiB row/analysis
and256MiB unique-content budgets are separate scopes, not a shared native-RSS
ceiling. Artifact's unchanged complete three-file64MiB check still applies.

admit/2 receives one borrowed value and current bounded tuple. scan first
checks scalar byte_size before UTF-8 traversal, container cardinality before
copying/enumerating, and bounded recursive depth/node counts; improper lists,
non-byte bitstrings, opaque pid/port/reference/function values, unsupported keys
and normalized-key collisions fail closed. No arbitrary Inspect/String.Chars
or exception formatter is invoked. Atom/binary/numeric/list/tuple/map values
retain the current json_value representation: nil/booleans stay primitives,
other atoms become strings, tuples become ordered arrays, invalid UTF-8 binary
values retain the existing {"binary_base64":...} wrapper. Supported map keys
are primitive binary/atom/integer/finite-float keys with their current primitive
String.Chars spellings (nil's key spelling is empty), implemented without that
protocol dispatch; composite keys fail. Distinct keys that normalize to the
same JSON key fail instead of allowing Map.new to discard one. Charge the
running raw scalar-byte lower bound before UTF-8/escape traversal, then the
exact compact contribution before continuing into the next value. Compute the exact
base64 expansion and escaped string/compact row length before conversion;
reject excess before allocating that expanded value. Check prospective total
nodes/bytes before retaining any list/map entry. After one independently
bounded candidate is normalized, charge its actual :erlang.external_size
before adding it to the retained corpus. That ETF logical measure is not heap
size; one bounded candidate can coexist during that check, not an unbounded
whole corpus. Rejected/unsupported input fails the collection, never substitutes
an opaque/truncated marker, silently drops a tag/case or changes source scope.

admit_case/2 checks the next row count before normalization/retention.
runtime_header(test, module, source_root) guards the borrowed native test/tags
shape, file/name/line types and scalar bounds before Path/atom/string identity
construction; it returns the existing physical path/line/module/name header.
Neither helper guesses declaration identity or changes the NUL-separated
case/runtime hash formulas. Admit the combined identity components before
constructing the NUL-joined hash input, not only each scalar separately.
Bounded count walks stop at limit+1 before sorting
or building another complete list; __ex_unit__/0's original returned list may
already exist and is not claimed to be produced under our allocation budget.

Exact prospective existing collector edits: require the new helper; run/1

No per-row budget reset or unchecked conversion entry survives. Retained

Replace write_json/2 with private write_json_pair/3 delegating to
the measured exclusive pair writer; load/1 becomes private load/2 so successful
and blocked result owners share admission. failure(file, kind, reason, trace,

read_input/4(root, relative_path, role, input_budget) owns existing Data bounded

decode or source-copy ownership. Return {bytes, next_input_budget}; each read
occurrence is charged, not just unique filenames. The frozen manifest is
digest-checked before its existing decode. A bounded8MiB review decode is not
claimed to enforce a parser-depth/native-RSS ceiling; the producer's metadata
depth/node gate precedes conversion and corpus retention of the decoded value.
Supplement copies are exclusive and exact reviewed paths. No source-input
cache, extra subprocess or revised git/source origin authority is proposed.
Existing git show/rev-parse/diff stdout and original source compilation remain

measure_pair/2 walks the complete normalized schema1 registration/schema2
details graphs, counting **every repeated serialized occurrence**, all key/
scalar escapes, punctuation and trailing newlines before opening either output.
It independently rechecks row counts/non-execution fields/fixed budgets rather

**P-CAP1 refinement after actual A-N32 and P-CAP3/4/5 reads.** Root assigned
only this existing-note refinement. Read the complete performance review from
2452, the recorded architecture A-N32 at3459 and its exact direct clarification,
and re-read current collector/snapshot/reserve bodies. Those findings are
accepted contract blockers in the first proposal, not implemented fixes or
runtime failures. The following precise dataflow supersedes conflicting earlier
API/retention wording. No helper/function/test/fixture/log/process was created,
and no original VM/collector/profile command or accounting change occurred.

Select admit_case({test,module,source_root},budget), returning
{normalized_raw_row,next_budget}. Its first operation validates the budget and
checks runtime_cases+1<=100_000; it commits that increment exactly once before
calling runtime_header/3, converting atoms/paths/numbers or allocating a raw
row. Failed collection never reuses an earlier budget or continues with an
unreported failed row. runtime_header/3 first checks native test/tags/map shape,
scalar byte bounds, name/module atom shapes and line1..u64MAX; only then may it
convert module/name/line or normalize the file path. It checks the prospective
combined header/row size before construction. Complete tags and header are
one bounded row intake; there is no caller-created row or independent unbudgeted
tag conversion before admit_case. Its returned raw row keeps every supported
tag and the same source_path/source_line/module/name fields.

WorkflowCompilerLineage.snapshot/0 does not call private bounded_count/2 or
admit_case. Inline existing snapshot/0 uses an anonymous tail-recursive proper-
list prefix walk (an ordinary function value, no new named helper/module) that
stops at limit+1 or an improper tail. It bounds selected module-version rows
at10_000 before group/map/sort, preserving all newer/unproved version headers.
For each selected current module, call __ex_unit__/0 once, then bound that
borrowed native tests list against the remaining100_000 runtime slots before
mapping it. For each row, existing reserve(:runtime_count,1,limit) executes
immediately BEFORE runtime_header(test,module,fetch(:root)); then construct
the existing consumer_proof_id field, reserve its actual existing row metadata
charge, then reserve the actual singleton [row] charge before retaining each
registration-list cons. At completion reserve actual full registered-list
external_size once before returning it in the proof. That repeated full-row/
list charge is explicit in the existing compiler metadata profile, not charged
to a reset collector budget. Existing proof/module/content views retain their
separate earlier Data boundaries; this note does not certify native snapshot
index storage or arbitrary compiler callbacks. No second runtime increment or
new public list API.
Native __ex_unit__/0/all_loaded list allocation and compiler callback work are
outside this intake guarantee. Existing content/analysis/version/sequence
provenance remains unchanged; this helper grants no source identity.

New helper API additions requested explicitly: retain/3 and cons/3 in the
same proposed WorkflowCaseMetadata, no new file/model/type/alias. Thus the
exact proposed public set is limits/0, new_budget/0, admit/2, admit_case/2,
runtime_header/3, failure/5, retain/3, cons/3, measure_pair/2, write_pair/4;
the twelve previously named private functions keep their exact names/arities.
retain(value,role,budget) returns {value,next_budget} without normalization or
protocol dispatch. Roles are fixed :row, :index, :container, :root and
:spine_fragment, not a caller-defined limit. It performs a bounded primitive
structure/depth/node walk, then calls actual :erlang.external_size(value) and
charges that returned maximum-ETF estimate before the candidate joins the
retained corpus. :row has the16MiB compact object gate; completed containers,
indexes and roots have remaining4M-node/64MiB aggregate gates, never the16MiB
whole-object gate. Scalar16MiB and depth96 still apply. Index-only tuple keys/
atom fields are bounded primitive data and never normalized or serialized by
this function; unknown opaque terms remain rejected. Output graphs separately
must consist entirely of normalized JSON values. One count-bounded container
candidate can coexist with already admitted referenced values during its actual
charge; this is explicit, not an allocation-before-construction or RSS proof.

cons(head,owned_tail,budget) returns {[head|owned_tail],next_budget}. Before
that prefix is retained, scan/charge the actual singleton [head] as
:spine_fragment; it includes the complete head term and one actual cons/end
structure. The tail comes only from this collector's previously charged empty
list/cons flow, never a borrowed untrusted native list. This is a deliberately
repeated logical full-head charge, not the external_size of the entire new
prefix and not proof of another physical payload copy. It bounds prospective
growth one cell at a time without re-walking owned_tail. At completion, retain
the ACTUAL full list once as :container before joining it to another owner.
That second full-term charge is explicit; no shallow term stands for a full
container. No ever-growing list/map prefix is repeatedly measured.

Node accounting is explicit: every bounded scan visit to a scalar/map/key/
tuple/list-cons/list-end contributes to the aggregate4M scanner-work counter;
the normalization scan also accounts for prospectively added binary_base64
map/key/string nodes before conversion. Repeated row/head/container/root scans
charge repeated visits. Normalization and external_size each make an additional
bounded pass over the admitted candidate; these are declared work occurrences,
not a strict CPU/RSS metric or an uncharged arbitrary traversal. The independent
pair premeasurement counts every normalized serialized occurrence under its
own8M/depth96/64MiB profile; emission is a second bounded occurrence walk.
Enclosing row/root structure can exceed depth even when tags alone fit, so
complete measurement must recheck it. No recorded budget tuple is authority
for external capture admission; current pure Artifact verification is mandatory.

Exact retained-owner sequence inside existing WorkflowCaseCollector.run/1:

- Keep one metadata budget from new_budget/0 across all success, blocked,
  profile, raw-row, index, derived-row and output-root branches. Never reset
  it per module, helper, root or stage. Bound manifest files/declarations and
  additional review files/roots to20_000 with a proper-list limit+1 pass before
  map/filter/group/merge/uniq/sort. Bound combined attempted helper/root source
  entries before concatenation; deduplication cannot rescue an over-limit
  attempted list. Counts are not inferred from a later read.
- Charge the already bounded/hash-verified decoded manifest/review as actual
  primitive :index owners. Extracted file/helper/root/ID entry objects use
  bounded admit or retain(:row); each new accumulator uses charged empty
  list plus cons/3, then one completed :container charge. Before sort/reverse,
  precheck cardinality and reserve an additional actual full-source-list
  external_size charge for the prospective permutation; afterwards compare
  the actual output-list external_size to that charge before retaining it.
  These are map/binary/atom lists, with unchanged elements and encoded sizes,
  not integer-list/string optimization cases. Each result/list is measured
  once, not once for every growing prefix. Std sorting's internal transient
  storage is not a claimed native-memory bound.
- Manifest grouping uses bounded declaration rows and key tuples. Charge each
  new key/one-entry map and bucket cons as an actual bounded fragment before
  map insertion; do not charge a growing whole bucket/map on every insertion.
  At completion retain the actual full grouping :index once. The tuple-key
  lookup and registered-ID set each retain their complete actual index once
  before use; per-entry and set candidate counts are prechecked. These indexes
  may borrow source strings/rows, but their repeated logical payload charge
  is explicit. No cache-hit or shared-reference zero charge is assumed.
- Initial/final selected test-module lists are each bounded at10_000 before
  another selected list is retained/sorted/subtracted. Native module/header
  entries and initial-module lookup set receive actual :index/container
  charges. For each selected module, prefix-bound native tests against remaining
  runtime slots before mapping, then use native-tuple admit_case for each row.
  Raw accumulation uses cons/3 and the completed raw/sorted-list owner gates.
- Split raw rows in one bounded walk. Exact matched/unmatched list spines get
  their cons/completed-container charges while tag terms may remain shared.
  For a matched row, precheck declaration ID and the exact identity-input byte
  sum for exactly [commit,ID,source_path,decimal_line,module,name], with five
  NUL separators,
  against16MiB BEFORE numeric conversion/Enum.join. Admit the bounded joined
  string as another owned temporary before hashing; retain its64-byte digest
  and declaration ID owners, then retain the actual final extended matched
  map as :row before adding it to the matched list. Discard the joined temporary
  after hashing, without refunding the cumulative logical/work budget.
- Helper/root load successes and complete blocked result maps are admitted
  individually; result-list spines and every helper-profile map entry/full map
  receive fragment/completed-owner charges. failure/5 scans kind/reason/stack,
  checks the full diagnostic's escaped compact bytes including its enclosure
  before constructing the diagnostic string, then charges that actual string.
  The blocked result map is a separate actual :row charge. Later blocker
  path+separator+diagnostic length and escaped contribution are checked before
  joining, followed by a separate string admission and blocker-list charges.
  Logical charges are repeated even when this string remains shared in details.
- Unresolved IDs, registered-ID membership, all warning/blocker strings,
  additional-root/file/ref maps and module/case/count fields have the same
  admitted leaf/entry and completed container/index ownership sequence. Each
  final registration/details root map is built once from those bounded owners,
  retained once as ACTUAL :root (including its full referenced payload), then
  independently measured as the complete pair. No whole graph is normalized
  through16MiB, and no repeatedly growing graph prefix is scanned. The larger
  repeated64MiB logical charges are conservative prospective limits, not proof
  that all original required cases fit; an excess fails the whole collection.

One input tuple {read_occurrences,read_bytes} survives all collector-owned
manifest/source/review/tool/helper/lock reads. Exactly private read_input/4
checks the next occurrence and remaining256MiB before its role-capped Data
read, then charges actual returned bytes. additional_sources/3 returns
{roots,sources,review,next_input_budget}; run/1 threads it onward through repeated
helper hashes, mix.lock and collector/tool hashes. No per-helper/review/source
reset, unique-file discount or early unbounded File.read remains in that owned
path. Compiler callback source/content reads and Artifact's own tool/content
reads retain their separately recorded profiles; these are additional scopes,
not concealed reads certified by the collector tuple. Original Code.require
and git subprocess internals likewise remain independent VM/process gates.

Exact measurement/output return shapes: measure_pair(registration,details)
returns {registration_bytes,details_bytes,pair_nodes}, each count including its
newline and the combined count<=64MiB. write_pair/4 returns
{{registration_relative_path,registration_bytes,registration_sha256},
 {details_relative_path,details_bytes,details_sha256}} ONLY after both measured
emissions and checked File.close results are successful. No authority/status
flag is returned. Any measurement, open, returned/raised write or close failure
fails the operation; no successful counts/hashes are returned for partial data.

open_pair/2 must record the first acquired {path,handle} immediately in a
protected lexical try BEFORE attempting the second exclusive open, and similarly
record the second before any write. The ordinary tracked state is
{{reg_path,reg_handle},{details_path_or_nil,details_handle_or_nil}}. After a
checked successful close replace only that handle with nil while retaining
the owned path until operation success. Nil path means never acquired,
distinct from an owned path with a closed handle; it is not a new model or
capture validity flag. emit_chunk/3 covers raised IO.binwrite failures as well as other
exceptions/throws/exits through rescue/catch. finish_or_cleanup/3 attempts every
owned handle close and owned path removal on failure without an earlier cleanup
error skipping the remaining owned resource. After cleanup, re-raise the original
kind/reason/stack when cleanup succeeded; on cleanup failure raise the ordinary
Erlang error tuple {:metadata_pair_cleanup_failed,{kind,reason},cleanup_results},
with at most two close and two unlink result entries and no protocol formatting.
It is failed-operation data, never an accepted diagnostic/JSON field. Both
successful close results are checked before the success tuple. A close failure
also triggers own-file cleanup. Never remove a preexisting path or directory.
Forced kill remains the separately owned original-stage/supervisor obligation.

```python
snapshot_read_bytes = 0

def snapshot_preflight_inputs():
    global snapshot_read_bytes
    for root in (ROOT, E, R):
        assert stat.S_ISDIR(root.lstat().st_mode)
        for parent in root.parents:
            assert stat.S_ISDIR(parent.lstat().st_mode) and not parent.is_symlink()
    assert not os.path.lexists(pathlib.Path(os.environ['HOME']) / '.erlang')
    assert not os.path.lexists(ROOT / '.erlang')
    paths = set(PINNED)
    paths.add(str(NOTE))
    for root in (E, R):
        for pattern in ('lib/**/ebin/*.beam', 'lib/**/ebin/*.app', 'lib/**/priv/lib/*.so'):
            for path in root.glob(pattern):
                text = str(path)
                assert len(text.encode('utf-8')) <= 4096
                if text not in paths:
                    assert len(paths) < 4096
                    paths.add(text)
    for path in (
        E / 'lib/elixir/lib/system.ex', E / 'lib/elixir/lib/json.ex',
        R / 'releases/RELEASES', R / 'releases/29/installed_application_versions',
        R / 'bin/start.boot', R / 'releases/29/start_clean.script',
    ):
        text = str(path)
        assert text in paths or len(paths) < 4096
        paths.add(text)
    assert len(paths) <= 4096
    snapshot = {}
    metadata_bytes = 2
    for text in sorted(paths):
        assert len(text.encode('utf-8')) <= 4096
        path = pathlib.Path(text)
        for parent in path.parents:
            assert stat.S_ISDIR(parent.lstat().st_mode) and not parent.is_symlink()
        info = path.lstat()
        assert stat.S_ISREG(info.st_mode) and 0 <= info.st_size <= 32 * 1024 * 1024
        assert info.st_size <= 256 * 1024 * 1024 - snapshot_read_bytes
        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NOFOLLOW | os.O_CLOEXEC)
        try:
            opened = os.fstat(fd)
            assert stat.S_ISREG(opened.st_mode)
            assert (opened.st_dev, opened.st_ino, opened.st_size, opened.st_mtime_ns, opened.st_ctime_ns) == (
                info.st_dev, info.st_ino, info.st_size, info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_ctime_ns)
            size, digest = 0, hashlib.sha256()
            while size < info.st_size:
                count = min(65536, info.st_size - size, 256 * 1024 * 1024 - snapshot_read_bytes)
                assert count > 0
                chunk = os.read(fd, count)
                snapshot_read_bytes += len(chunk)
                size += len(chunk)
                assert chunk, 'short EOF before known input size'
                digest.update(chunk)
            final = os.fstat(fd)
            assert (final.st_dev, final.st_ino, size, final.st_size, final.st_mtime_ns, final.st_ctime_ns) == (
                info.st_dev, info.st_ino, info.st_size, info.st_size, info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_ctime_ns)
        finally:
            os.close(fd)
        sha256 = digest.hexdigest()
        if text in PINNED:
            assert sha256 == PINNED[text], text
        row = {'type': 'regular', 'bytes': size, 'sha256': sha256}
        fragment = json.dumps({text: row}, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')
        metadata_bytes += len(fragment) - (2 if not snapshot else 1)
        assert metadata_bytes <= 512 * 1024
        snapshot[text] = row
    assert len(json.dumps(snapshot, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')) == metadata_bytes
    return snapshot

def terminate_preflight_group(process):
    term_end = time.monotonic() + 2
    kill_end = term_end + 2
    try:
        os.killpg(process.pid, signal.SIGTERM)
    except ProcessLookupError:
        process.wait(timeout=max(0, term_end - time.monotonic()))
        return True
    while time.monotonic() < term_end:
        process.poll()
        try:
            os.killpg(process.pid, 0)
        except ProcessLookupError:
            process.wait(timeout=max(0, term_end - time.monotonic()))
            return True
        time.sleep(min(0.02, max(0, term_end - time.monotonic())))
    try:
        os.killpg(process.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
    except ProcessLookupError:
        pass
    while time.monotonic() < kill_end:
        process.poll()
        try:
            os.killpg(process.pid, 0)
        except ProcessLookupError:
            process.wait(timeout=max(0, kill_end - time.monotonic()))
            return True
        time.sleep(min(0.02, max(0, kill_end - time.monotonic())))
    return False

def run_cached_preflight(argv, environment):
    buffers = {'stdout': bytearray(), 'stderr': bytearray()}
    process, selector, failure, gone = None, None, None, False
    deadline = time.monotonic() + 10
    try:
        process = subprocess.Popen(argv, cwd=ROOT, env=environment, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,
                                   stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, start_new_session=True)
        selector = selectors.DefaultSelector()
        for name, stream in (('stdout', process.stdout), ('stderr', process.stderr)):
            os.set_blocking(stream.fileno(), False)
            selector.register(stream, selectors.EVENT_READ, name)
        while selector.get_map() or process.poll() is None:
            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if remaining <= 0:
                raise RuntimeError('preflight10second deadline exceeded')
            for key, _ in selector.select(min(0.1, remaining)):
                chunk = os.read(key.fileobj.fileno(), 4096)
                if not chunk:
                    selector.unregister(key.fileobj)
                    key.fileobj.close()
                    continue
                buffer = buffers[key.data]
                if len(chunk) > 65536 - len(buffer):
                    raise RuntimeError(key.data + ' exceeds64KiB actual stream limit')
                buffer.extend(chunk)
        process.wait(timeout=max(0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
    except BaseException as error:
        failure = type(error).__name__ + ': ' + str(error)[:1024]
    finally:
        if process is None:
            gone = True
        else:
            try:
                gone = terminate_preflight_group(process)
            except BaseException as error:
                gone = False
                if failure is None:
                    failure = 'cleanup ' + type(error).__name__ + ': ' + str(error)[:1024]
        if selector is not None:
            try:
                selector.close()
            except BaseException as error:
                if failure is None:
                    failure = 'selector close ' + type(error).__name__ + ': ' + str(error)[:1024]
        if process is not None:
            for stream in (process.stdout, process.stderr):
                if stream is not None and not stream.closed:
                    try:
                        stream.close()
                    except BaseException as error:
                        if failure is None:
                            failure = 'pipe close ' + type(error).__name__ + ': ' + str(error)[:1024]
    return {'pid': None if process is None else process.pid,
            'exit': None if process is None else process.returncode,
            'failure': failure, 'group_gone': gone, 'output_encoding': 'hex',
            'stdout': bytes(buffers['stdout']).hex(), 'stderr': bytes(buffers['stderr']).hex()}

def unique_json_object(pairs):
    result = {}
    for key, value in pairs:
        if key in result:
            raise ValueError('duplicate JSON object key')
        result[key] = value
    return result

started_before = Application.started_applications() |> Enum.map(fn {app, _, _} -> Atom.to_string(app) end) |> Enum.sort()
true = started_before == ["compiler", "elixir", "kernel", "logger", "stdlib"]
Code.ensure_loaded!(:crypto)
sha256_abc = Base.encode16(:crypto.hash(:sha256, "abc"), case: :lower)
true = sha256_abc == "ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad"
expected = JSON.decode!(~S|__EXPECTED_JSON__|)
true = length(expected) == 4
true = Enum.map(expected, & &1["module"]) == ["Elixir.System", "Elixir.JSON", "crypto", "json"]
cached_module_row = fn row ->
  module = String.to_existing_atom(row["module"])
  Code.ensure_loaded!(module)
  path_chars = :code.which(module)
  true = is_list(path_chars) and length(Enum.take(path_chars, 4097)) <= 4096
  path = path_chars |> List.to_string() |> Path.expand()
  true = byte_size(path) <= 4096 and path == row["path"]
  {^module, bytes, object_chars} = :code.get_object_code(module)
  true = is_binary(bytes) and byte_size(bytes) <= 32 * 1024 * 1024
  true = is_list(object_chars) and length(Enum.take(object_chars, 4097)) <= 4096
  true = (object_chars |> List.to_string() |> Path.expand()) == path
  sha256 = Base.encode16(:crypto.hash(:sha256, bytes), case: :lower)
  true = sha256 == row["sha256"]
  {:ok, {^module, loaded_md5}} = :beam_lib.md5(bytes)
  true = byte_size(loaded_md5) == 16 and module.module_info(:md5) == loaded_md5
  %{"module" => row["module"], "path" => path, "sha256" => sha256,
    "loaded_md5" => Base.encode16(loaded_md5, case: :lower)}
end
module_rows = Enum.map(expected, cached_module_row)
payload = %{"empty" => [], "text" => "é\n\"\\", "values" => [nil, true, false, 0, 18_446_744_073_709_551_615]}
json_encoded = JSON.encode!(payload)
true = byte_size(json_encoded) <= 1024
json_decoded = JSON.decode!(json_encoded)
true = json_decoded === payload
libraries = Enum.take(:crypto.info_lib(), 5)
true = length(libraries) in 1..4
library_rows = Enum.map(libraries, fn {name, version, text} ->
  true = is_binary(name) and byte_size(name) <= 256 and String.valid?(name)
  true = is_integer(version) and version >= 0 and version <= 18_446_744_073_709_551_615
  true = is_binary(text) and byte_size(text) <= 256 and String.valid?(text)
  %{"name" => name, "version" => version, "text" => text}
end)
false = has_registered_test_module.()
started_after = Application.started_applications() |> Enum.map(fn {app, _, _} -> Atom.to_string(app) end) |> Enum.sort()
true = started_after == started_before
mix_modules_after = for module <- mix_names, into: %{}, do: {Atom.to_string(module), :code.is_loaded(module) != false}
mix_processes_after = for module <- mix_names, into: %{}, do: {Atom.to_string(module), Process.whereis(module) != nil}
true = Enum.all?(Map.values(mix_modules_after) ++ Map.values(mix_processes_after), &(&1 === false))
loaded_after = Enum.take(:code.all_loaded(), 4097)
true = length(loaded_after) <= 4096
true = Enum.all?(loaded_after, fn {module, origin} ->
  name = Atom.to_string(module)
  false = (name == "Elixir.Mix" or String.starts_with?(name, "Elixir.Mix."))
  case origin do
    :preloaded -> true
    path when is_list(path) ->
      true = length(Enum.take(path, 4097)) <= 4096
      value = path |> List.to_string() |> Path.expand()
      byte_size(value) <= 4096 and (String.starts_with?(value, e <> "/") or String.starts_with?(value, r <> "/"))
    _ -> false
  end
end)
pid = System.pid()
true = byte_size(pid) <= 64 and String.valid?(pid)
observation = %{
  "schema_version" => 1, "kind" => "installed-supported-runtime-preflight", "pid" => pid,
  "elixir_version" => System.version(), "build_info" => build_info, "otp_release" => otp_release,
  "erts_version" => erts_version, "architecture" => architecture, "os_type" => ["unix", "darwin"],
  "os_version" => [os_major, os_minor, os_patch], "code_root" => code_root, "modules" => module_rows,
  "sha256_abc" => sha256_abc, "json_encoded" => json_encoded, "json_decoded" => json_decoded,
  "crypto_libraries" => library_rows, "started_before" => started_before, "started_after" => started_after,
  "registered_modules_before" => false, "registered_modules_after" => false,
  "mix_modules_before" => mix_modules_before, "mix_modules_after" => mix_modules_after,
  "mix_processes_before" => mix_processes_before, "mix_processes_after" => mix_processes_after
}
frame = "WF_SUPPORTED_PREFLIGHT_V1 " <> JSON.encode!(observation) <> "\n"
true = byte_size(frame) <= 16384
:ok = IO.binwrite(:stdio, frame)
'''
assert expression.count('__EXPECTED_JSON__') == 1
expression = expression.replace('__EXPECTED_JSON__', expected_json)
assert len(expression.encode('utf-8')) <= 32768
environment = {
    'PATH': str(R / 'bin') + ':/usr/bin:/bin', 'HOME': os.environ['HOME'],
    'TMPDIR': os.environ['TMPDIR'], 'LANG': 'C', 'LC_ALL': 'C', 'ERL_CRASH_DUMP_BYTES': '0',
}
argv = [
    '/bin/sh', '-c', 'ulimit -c 0 || exit; exec "$@"', 'wf-supported-preflight',
    str(E / 'bin/elixir'), '--no-color', '--erl',
    '+S 2:2 +SDcpu 1 +SDio 1 +A 1 -noinput -boot ' + str(R / 'releases/29/start_clean'),
    '-e', expression,
]
before_digest = hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(before, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')).hexdigest()
pre_record = ('\nR-SUP1 exact assigned candidate-only source/argv before sole VM\n' +
              json.dumps({'program_source': program_source, 'program_sha256': program_sha256,
                          'expression': expression, 'argv': argv, 'environment': environment,
                          'before': before, 'before_sha256': before_digest},
                         sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')) + '\n').encode('ascii')
post_reserve = 512 * 1024 + 2 * 65536 * 2 + 65536
assert len(pre_record) + post_reserve <= 2 * 1024 * 1024
fd = os.open(LOG, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_APPEND | os.O_NOFOLLOW | os.O_CLOEXEC)
try:
    trace_info = os.fstat(fd)
    assert stat.S_ISREG(trace_info.st_mode)
    assert trace_info.st_size + len(pre_record) + post_reserve <= 8 * 1024 * 1024
    trace_identity = (trace_info.st_dev, trace_info.st_ino)
    offset = 0
    while offset < len(pre_record):
        count = os.write(fd, pre_record[offset:offset + 65536])
        assert count > 0
        offset += count
finally:
    os.close(fd)
result = run_cached_preflight(argv, environment)
after, after_failure = {}, None
try:
    after = snapshot_preflight_inputs()
except BaseException as error:
    after_failure = type(error).__name__ + ': ' + str(error)[:1024]
result['inventory_unchanged'] = after_failure is None and before == after
result['inventory_count'] = len(before)
result['inventory_sha256'] = before_digest
result['after_failure'] = after_failure
result['frame_count'] = 0
try:
    assert result['exit'] == 0 and result['failure'] is None and result['group_gone']
    assert result['inventory_unchanged'] and result['output_encoding'] == 'hex'
    stdout, stderr = bytes.fromhex(result['stdout']), bytes.fromhex(result['stderr'])
    assert stderr == b'' and len(stdout) <= 16384
    prefix = b'WF_SUPPORTED_PREFLIGHT_V1 '
    assert stdout.startswith(prefix) and stdout.endswith(b'\n') and stdout.count(b'\n') == 1
    result['frame_count'] = 1
    frame = json.loads(stdout[len(prefix):-1].decode('utf-8', 'strict'), object_pairs_hook=unique_json_object)
    assert set(frame) == {
        'schema_version', 'kind', 'pid', 'elixir_version', 'build_info', 'otp_release', 'erts_version',
        'architecture', 'os_type', 'os_version', 'code_root', 'modules', 'sha256_abc',
        'json_encoded', 'json_decoded', 'crypto_libraries', 'started_before', 'started_after',
        'registered_modules_before', 'registered_modules_after',
        'mix_modules_before', 'mix_modules_after', 'mix_processes_before', 'mix_processes_after'}
    assert type(frame['schema_version']) is int and frame['schema_version'] == 1
    assert (frame['kind'], frame['pid'], frame['elixir_version'], frame['otp_release'],
            frame['erts_version'], frame['code_root']) == (
        'installed-supported-runtime-preflight', str(result['pid']), '1.20.4', '29', '17.0.5', str(R))
    build = frame['build_info']
    assert set(build) == {'build', 'date', 'otp_release', 'revision', 'version'}
    assert all(type(value) is str and len(value.encode('utf-8')) <= 4096 for value in build.values())
    assert build['version'] == '1.20.4' and build['otp_release'] == '29'
    assert type(frame['architecture']) is str and len(frame['architecture'].encode('utf-8')) <= 4096
    assert frame['architecture'].startswith(('aarch64-apple-darwin', 'arm64-apple-darwin'))
    assert frame['os_type'] == ['unix', 'darwin']
    assert type(frame['os_version']) is list and len(frame['os_version']) == 3
    assert all(type(value) is int and 0 <= value <= 18446744073709551615 for value in frame['os_version'])
    assert frame['os_version'] == [int(part) for part in os.uname().release.split('.')]
    assert len(frame['modules']) == 4
    for expected, actual in zip(modules, frame['modules']):
        assert set(actual) == {'module', 'path', 'sha256', 'loaded_md5'}
        assert {key: actual[key] for key in expected} == expected
        assert type(actual['loaded_md5']) is str and len(actual['loaded_md5']) == 32
        assert all(character in '0123456789abcdef' for character in actual['loaded_md5'])
    assert frame['sha256_abc'] == hashlib.sha256(b'abc').hexdigest()
    payload = {'empty': [], 'text': 'é\n"\\', 'values': [None, True, False, 0, 18446744073709551615]}
    assert type(frame['json_encoded']) is str and len(frame['json_encoded'].encode('utf-8')) <= 1024
    for decoded in (json.loads(frame['json_encoded'], object_pairs_hook=unique_json_object), frame['json_decoded']):
        assert type(decoded) is dict and set(decoded) == {'empty', 'text', 'values'}
        assert type(decoded['empty']) is list and decoded['empty'] == []
        assert type(decoded['text']) is str and decoded['text'] == payload['text']
        assert type(decoded['values']) is list and len(decoded['values']) == 5
        assert [type(value) for value in decoded['values']] == [type(None), bool, bool, int, int]
        assert decoded['values'] == payload['values']
    assert 1 <= len(frame['crypto_libraries']) <= 4
    for row in frame['crypto_libraries']:
        assert set(row) == {'name', 'version', 'text'}
        assert all(type(row[key]) is str and len(row[key].encode('utf-8')) <= 256 for key in ('name', 'text'))
        assert type(row['version']) is int and 0 <= row['version'] <= 18446744073709551615
    assert frame['started_before'] == frame['started_after'] == ['compiler', 'elixir', 'kernel', 'logger', 'stdlib']
    assert frame['registered_modules_before'] is False and frame['registered_modules_after'] is False
    mix_names = {'Elixir.Mix', 'Elixir.Mix.Supervisor', 'Elixir.Mix.PubSub', 'Elixir.Mix.Sync.PubSub'}
    for field in ('mix_modules_before', 'mix_modules_after', 'mix_processes_before', 'mix_processes_after'):
        assert set(frame[field]) == mix_names and all(value is False for value in frame[field].values())
    result['accepted'] = True
except BaseException as error:
    result['accepted'] = False
    result['parent_validation_failure'] = type(error).__name__ + ': ' + str(error)[:1024]
post_record = ('\nR-SUP1 candidate-only actual result; no cached/project/source acceptance\n' +
               json.dumps({'result': result, 'after': after, 'input_read_bytes': snapshot_read_bytes},
                          sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')) + '\n').encode('ascii')
assert len(post_record) <= post_reserve and len(pre_record) + len(post_record) <= 2 * 1024 * 1024
fd = os.open(LOG, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_APPEND | os.O_NOFOLLOW | os.O_CLOEXEC)
try:
    trace_after = os.fstat(fd)
    assert stat.S_ISREG(trace_after.st_mode) and (trace_after.st_dev, trace_after.st_ino) == trace_identity
    assert trace_after.st_size == trace_info.st_size + len(pre_record)
    assert trace_after.st_size + len(post_record) <= 8 * 1024 * 1024
    offset = 0
    while offset < len(post_record):
        count = os.write(fd, post_record[offset:offset + 65536])
        assert count > 0
        offset += count
finally:
    os.close(fd)
print(json.dumps({key: result[key] for key in (
    'pid', 'exit', 'failure', 'group_gone', 'inventory_unchanged', 'inventory_count',
    'inventory_sha256', 'frame_count', 'accepted')}))
if not result['accepted']:
    raise SystemExit(1)
```

```python
    'os_type', 'os_version', 'code_root', 'modules', 'sha256_abc', 'json_encoded',
    'json_decoded', 'crypto_libraries',
}
BUILD_INFO_KEYS = {'build', 'date', 'otp_release', 'revision', 'version'}
CONFIG_KEYS = {
    'imports', 'runtime_imports', 'config_bytes', 'runtime_bytes',
    'environment_bytes', 'config_sha256', 'runtime_sha256', 'selected',
}
SELECTED_KEYS = {
    'ports', 'workers', 'force_build', 'nif_skip', 'topologies_disabled',
    'data_dir', 'data_dir_auto_cleanup',
}
LOCK_KEYS = {'uid', 'port', 'key', 'path', 'active', 'released'}
MIX_PROCESSES = [
    'Elixir.Mix.Supervisor', 'Elixir.Mix.Sync.PubSub', 'Elixir.Mix.State',
    'Elixir.Mix.TasksServer', 'Elixir.Mix.ProjectStack', 'Elixir.Mix.PubSub',
    'Elixir.Mix.PubSub.Subscriber', 'Elixir.Mix.PubSub.ListenerSupervisor',
]

def snapshot_preflight_inputs():
    global owner_work_bytes, snapshot_read_bytes, owner_work_limit
    assert 131072 <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
    owner_work_bytes += 131072
    # One bounded two-chunk peak; chunks are hashed immediately and never retained in rows.
    files, directories, raw_members = {}, {}, {}
    paths, seen_descendants = set(PINNED), set()
    metadata_bytes, entry_count, directory_count = 128, 0, 0
    raw_name_bytes, joined_path_bytes, maximum_depth, match_count = 0, 0, 0, 0
    matches = {'guides': [], 'docs': []}
    paths.add(str(NOTE))
    for root in (ROOT, E, R, B):
        for parent in (root, *root.parents):
            text = str(parent)
            assert len(text.encode('utf-8', 'strict')) <= 4096
            info = parent.lstat()
            assert stat.S_ISDIR(info.st_mode) and not stat.S_ISLNK(info.st_mode)
            header = ['directory', info.st_dev, info.st_ino, info.st_mode, info.st_uid,
                      info.st_nlink, info.st_size, info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_ctime_ns]
            if text in directories:
                assert directories[text] == header
                continue
            assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) < 4096
            charge = 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
            assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
            owner_work_bytes += charge
            fragment = json.dumps({text: header}, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')
            metadata_bytes += len(fragment) + 1
            assert metadata_bytes <= 524288
            directories[text] = header
    for absent in (pathlib.Path('/Users/yoavgea/.erlang'), ROOT / '.erlang',
                   B / '.erlang', B / 'erl_crash.dump'):
        assert not os.path.lexists(absent)
        text = str(absent)
        assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) < 4096
        assert 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8')) <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
        owner_work_bytes += 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
        raw_members[text] = None
        metadata_bytes += 8 + 6 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
        assert metadata_bytes <= 524288
    for root in (E, R):
        for pattern in ('lib/**/ebin/*.beam', 'lib/**/ebin/*.app', 'lib/**/priv/lib/*.so'):
            for path in root.glob(pattern):
                text = str(path)
                assert len(text.encode('utf-8', 'strict')) <= 4096
                if text not in paths:
                    assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) < 4096
                    charge = 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
                    assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
                    owner_work_bytes += charge
                    paths.add(text)
    for path in (E / 'lib/elixir/lib/system.ex', E / 'lib/elixir/lib/json.ex',
                 R / 'releases/RELEASES', R / 'releases/29/installed_application_versions',
                 R / 'bin/start.boot', R / 'releases/29/start_clean.script'):
        text = str(path)
        assert text in paths or len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) < 4096
        assert 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8')) <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
        owner_work_bytes += 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
        paths.add(text)
    pending = [(B, -1), (B / 'guides', 0), (B / 'docs', 0)]
    while pending:
        directory, depth = pending.pop()
        text = str(directory)
        assert depth <= 16 and len(text.encode('utf-8', 'strict')) <= 4096
        if not os.path.lexists(directory):
            assert directory in (B / 'guides', B / 'docs')
            assert 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8')) <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
            owner_work_bytes += 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
            raw_members[text] = None
            metadata_bytes += 8 + 6 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
            assert metadata_bytes <= 524288
            continue
        info = directory.lstat()
        assert stat.S_ISDIR(info.st_mode) and not stat.S_ISLNK(info.st_mode)
        identity = (info.st_dev, info.st_ino)
        assert identity not in seen_descendants
        assert directory_count < 128
        directory_count += 1
        maximum_depth = max(maximum_depth, max(depth, 0))
        assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) < 4096
        assert 4096 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8')) <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
        owner_work_bytes += 4096 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
        seen_descendants.add(identity)
        header = ['directory', info.st_dev, info.st_ino, info.st_mode, info.st_uid,
                  info.st_nlink, info.st_size, info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_ctime_ns]
        if text in directories:
            assert directories[text] == header
        else:
            directories[text] = header
            metadata_bytes += 256 + 6 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
        # Native listdir returns names before admission; this is not a native heap quota.
        names = os.listdir(directory)
        assert len(names) <= 256 and entry_count + len(names) <= 1024
        charge = 1024 + 256 * len(names)
        assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
        owner_work_bytes += charge
        names = sorted(names)
        rows = []
        for name in names:
            name_bytes = name.encode('utf-8', 'strict')
            assert name not in ('', '.', '..') and '/' not in name and '\0' not in name
            assert len(name_bytes) <= 255 and entry_count < 1024
            entry = directory / name
            value = str(entry)
            value_bytes = value.encode('utf-8', 'strict')
            assert len(value_bytes) <= 4096
            assert raw_name_bytes + len(name_bytes) <= 131072
            assert joined_path_bytes + len(value_bytes) <= 262144
            assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) + entry_count < 4096
            entry_count += 1
            raw_name_bytes += len(name_bytes)
            joined_path_bytes += len(value_bytes)
            charge = 2048 + 16 * (len(name_bytes) + len(value_bytes))
            assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
            owner_work_bytes += charge
            entry_info = entry.lstat()
            assert stat.S_ISREG(entry_info.st_mode) or stat.S_ISDIR(entry_info.st_mode)
            assert not stat.S_ISLNK(entry_info.st_mode)
            if stat.S_ISREG(entry_info.st_mode):
                assert entry_info.st_nlink == 1
            kind = 'directory' if stat.S_ISDIR(entry_info.st_mode) else 'regular'
            entry_header = [kind, entry_info.st_dev, entry_info.st_ino, entry_info.st_mode,
                            entry_info.st_uid, entry_info.st_nlink, entry_info.st_size,
                            entry_info.st_mtime_ns, entry_info.st_ctime_ns]
            fragment = json.dumps([name, entry_header], separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')
            metadata_bytes += len(fragment) + 1
            assert metadata_bytes <= 524288
            rows.append([name, entry_header])
            if depth >= 0 and kind == 'directory':
                assert depth < 16
                pending.append((entry, depth + 1))
            if depth >= 0 and name.endswith('.md'):
                relative = entry.relative_to(B)
                visible = all(not part.startswith('.') for part in relative.parts)
                eligible = relative.parts[0] == 'docs' or depth == 0
                if visible and eligible:
                    key = relative.parts[0]
                    rendered = '../../' + relative.as_posix()
                    charge = 1024 + 16 * len(rendered.encode('utf-8'))
                    assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
                    owner_work_bytes += charge
                    matches[key].append(rendered)
                    match_count += 1
        raw_members[text] = rows
        final = directory.lstat()
        assert (final.st_dev, final.st_ino, final.st_mode, final.st_uid, final.st_nlink,
                final.st_size, final.st_mtime_ns, final.st_ctime_ns) == tuple(header[1:])
    for key in ('guides', 'docs'):
        assert 1024 + 256 * len(matches[key]) <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
        owner_work_bytes += 1024 + 256 * len(matches[key])
        matches[key] = sorted(matches[key])
    assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) + entry_count <= 4096
    charge = 2048 + 128 * len(paths)
    assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
    owner_work_bytes += charge
    for text in sorted(paths):
        assert len(text.encode('utf-8', 'strict')) <= 4096
        path = pathlib.Path(text)
        for parent in path.parents:
            value = str(parent)
            info = parent.lstat()
            assert stat.S_ISDIR(info.st_mode) and not stat.S_ISLNK(info.st_mode)
            header = ['directory', info.st_dev, info.st_ino, info.st_mode, info.st_uid,
                      info.st_nlink, info.st_size, info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_ctime_ns]
            if value in directories:
                assert directories[value] == header
            else:
                assert len(paths) + len(directories) + len(raw_members) + entry_count < 4096
                charge = 1024 + 16 * len(value.encode('utf-8', 'strict'))
                assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
                owner_work_bytes += charge
                fragment = json.dumps({value: header}, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')
                metadata_bytes += len(fragment) + 1
                assert metadata_bytes <= 524288
                directories[value] = header
        info = path.lstat()
        assert stat.S_ISREG(info.st_mode) and info.st_nlink == 1
        assert 0 <= info.st_size <= 33554432
        assert info.st_size <= 268435456 - snapshot_read_bytes
        charge = 1024 + 16 * len(text.encode('utf-8'))
        assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
        owner_work_bytes += charge
        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NOFOLLOW | os.O_CLOEXEC)
        read_error = None
        try:
            opened = os.fstat(fd)
            assert tuple(getattr(opened, field) for field in STAT_FIELDS) == tuple(
                getattr(info, field) for field in STAT_FIELDS)
            size, digest = 0, hashlib.sha256()
            while size < info.st_size:
                count = min(65536, info.st_size - size, 268435456 - snapshot_read_bytes)
                assert count > 0
                chunk = os.read(fd, count)
                snapshot_read_bytes += len(chunk)
                size += len(chunk)
                assert chunk, 'short EOF before admitted input size'
                digest.update(chunk)
            final = os.fstat(fd)
            assert tuple(getattr(final, field) for field in STAT_FIELDS) == tuple(
                getattr(info, field) for field in STAT_FIELDS)
        except BaseException as error:
            read_error = error
        finally:
            try:
                os.close(fd)
            except BaseException as close_error:
                if read_error is not None:
                    raise BaseExceptionGroup('input read and close', [read_error, close_error])
                raise
        if read_error is not None:
            raise read_error
        current = path.lstat()
        assert tuple(getattr(current, field) for field in STAT_FIELDS) == tuple(
            getattr(info, field) for field in STAT_FIELDS)
        sha256 = digest.hexdigest()
        if text in PINNED:
            assert sha256 == PINNED[text]
        row = ['regular', info.st_dev, info.st_ino, info.st_mode, info.st_uid,
               info.st_nlink, size, info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_ctime_ns, sha256]
        fragment = json.dumps({text: row}, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')
        metadata_bytes += len(fragment) + 1
        assert metadata_bytes <= 524288
        files[text] = row
    census = {
        'entries': entry_count, 'directories': directory_count, 'raw_name_bytes': raw_name_bytes,
        'joined_path_bytes': joined_path_bytes, 'maximum_depth': maximum_depth, 'matches': match_count,
    }
    charge = 3 * 524288 + 2048 + 256 * (len(files) + len(directories) + len(raw_members))
    assert charge <= owner_work_limit - owner_work_bytes
    owner_work_bytes += charge
    snapshot = {
        'files': files, 'directories': directories, 'raw_members': raw_members,
        'matches': matches, 'census': census,
    }
    encoded = json.dumps(snapshot, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode('ascii')
    assert len(encoded) <= metadata_bytes <= 524288
    return snapshot
```
